(posedge clk)\nif (rst) ptr_q <= 6'd0;\nelse if (img_start_i) ptr_q <= 6'd0;\nelse if (outport_valid_o) ptr_q <= ptr_q + 6'd1;\nassign outport_valid_o = valid_q[6];\nassign outport_data_o = block_out[ptr_q[2:0]];\nassign outport_idx_o = ptr_q;\nendmodule\n\n//-----------------------------------------------------------------\n// jpeg_idct_transpose: using 2 flat 64-entry arrays (one per block buffer)\n// with synchronous read. Read address = rd_idx_q directly.\n// Write: flat_buf[wr_block][inport_idx_i] <= data\n// Read: flat_buf[rd_block][rd_idx_q] (synchronous read)\n// The read order is column-major: rd_idx_q counts 0..63.\n// But the write order is row-major: inport_idx_i counts 0..63 row by row.\n// So the transpose naturally happens by writing row-major and reading sequential.\n// However, the y-pass expects 4 data lanes per beat, not 1.\n// The original transpose outputs 4 values per beat (from 4 different RAM banks).\n// So we need to output 4 values simultaneously.\n// The read order is: for each column, read 4 rows at once.\n// rd_idx_q goes 0,1,2,...,63. For each group of 8, we read a column.\n// The 4 data lanes correspond to 4 consecutive entries in the buffer.\n// Actually no - the 4 lanes are interleaved from 4 banks.\n// Let me just keep the 4-RAM structure but with 8-entry arrays per bank.\n//-----------------------------------------------------------------\nmodule jpeg_idct_transpose(\n    input           clk, input           rst, input           img_start_i, input           img_end_i,\n    input           inport_valid_i,\n    input  [31:0]   inport_data_i,\n    input  [5:0]    inport_idx_i,\n    input           outport_ready_i,\n    output          inport_accept_o,\n    output          outport_valid_o,\n    output [31:0]   outport_data0_o, output [31:0]   outport_data1_o,\n    output [31:0]   outport_data2_o, output [31:0]   outport_data3_o,\n    output [2:0]    outport_idx_o\n);\nreg         block_wr_q, block_rd_q;\nreg [5:0]   rd_idx_q;\nreg [3:0]   rd_addr_q;\n\nwire [4:0]  wr_ptr_w = {block_wr_q, inport_idx_i[3:0]};\nwire wr0_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd0);\nwire wr1_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd1);\nwire wr2_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd2);\nwire wr3_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd3);\n\nwire [4:0] rd_addr_w = {block_rd_q, rd_addr_q};\n\nreg [31:0] ram0 [0:31];\nreg [31:0] ram1 [0:31];\nreg [31:0] ram2 [0:31];\nreg [31:0] ram3 [0:31];\n\nreg [31:0] ram_read0_q, ram_read1_q, ram_read2_q, ram_read3_q;\n\nalways @ (posedge clk) begin\n    if (wr0_w) ram0[wr_ptr_w] <= inport_data_i;\n    ram_read0_q <= ram0[rd_addr_w];\nend\nalways @ (posedge clk) begin\n    if (wr1_w) ram1[wr_ptr_w] <= inport_data_i;\n    ram_read1_q <= ram1[rd_addr_w];\nend\nalways @ (posedge clk) begin\n    if (wr2_w) ram2[wr_ptr_w] <= inport_data_i;\n    ram_read2_q <= ram2[rd_addr_w];\nend\nalways @ (posedge clk) begin\n    if (wr3_w) ram3[wr_ptr_w] <= inport_data_i;\n    ram_read3_q <= ram3[rd_addr_w];\nend\n\nassign outport_data0_o = ram_read0_q;\nassign outport_data1_o = ram_read1_q;\nassign outport_data2_o = ram_read2_q;\nassign outport_data3_o = ram_read3_q;\n\nreg [1:0] block_ready_q;\nalways @ (posedge clk)\nif (rst) begin block_ready_q <= 0; block_wr_q <= 0; block_rd_q <= 0; end\nelse if (img_start_i) begin block_ready_q <= 0; block_wr_q <= 0; block_rd_q <= 0; end\nelse begin\n    if (inport_valid_i && inport_idx_i == 6'd63 && inport_accept_o) begin\n        block_ready_q[block_wr_q] <= 1'b1; block_wr_q <= ~block_wr_q;\n    end\n    if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q == 6'd63) begin\n        block_ready_q[block_rd_q] <= 1'b0; block_rd_q <= ~block_rd_q;\n    end\nend\nassign inport_accept_o = ~block_ready_q[block_wr_q];\n\nlocalparam STATE_IDLE = 2'd0, STATE_SETUP = 2'd1, STATE_ACTIVE = 2'd2;\nreg [1:0] state_q, next_state_r;\nalways @ * begin\n    next_state_r = state_q;\n    case (state_q)\n    STATE_IDLE:   if (block_ready_q[block_rd_q] && outport_ready_i) next_state_r = STATE_SETUP;\n    STATE_SETUP:  next_state_r = STATE_ACTIVE;\n    STATE_ACTIVE: if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q == 6'd63) next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;\n    default: ;\n    endcase\n    if (img_start_i) next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;\nend\nalways @ (posedge clk)\nif (rst) state_q <= STATE_IDLE;\nelse state_q <= next_state_r;\n\nalways @ (posedge clk)\nif (rst) rd_idx_q <= 6'b0;\nelse if (img_start_i) rd_idx_q <= 6'b0;\nelse if (state_q == STATE_ACTIVE) rd_idx_q <= rd_idx_q + 6'd1;\n\nalways @ (posedge clk)\nif (rst) rd_addr_q <= 4'b0;\nelse if (state_q == STATE_IDLE) rd_addr_q <= 4'b0;\nelse if (state_q == STATE_SETUP) rd_addr_q <= 4'd8;\nelse if (state_q == STATE_ACTIVE) begin\n    case (rd_idx_q[2:0])\n    3'd0: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q - 4'd8;\n    3'd1: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q + 4'd8;\n    3'd2: ;\n    3'd3: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q - 4'd8;\n    3'd4: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q + 4'd8;\n STATE_ACTIVE);\nassign outport_idx_o = rd_idx_q[2:0];\nendmodule\n\n//-----------------------------------------------------------------\n// jpeg_idct_y: column pass, OUT_SHIFT=15\n//-----------------------------------------------------------------\nmodule jpeg_idct_y\n#(parameter OUT_SHIFT = 15, parameter INPUT_WIDTH = 32)\n(\n    input           clk, input [ASSISTANT]
OK, let me go back to the working v3 and try a different strategy. The DFF QN delay is high because of the large fan-out. What if I use an inverting DFF and avoid the QN output? Or better yet, what if I store the address pre-combined and use a non-inverted output?

Actually, looking at the timing path more carefully: `rd_addr_q` QN → INV → NAND → HB1 → HB1 → OAI → INV → A2O1 → OAI → O2A1 → AO21 → HB1 → DFF. The path goes through 11 levels of logic. The 32:1 mux is the bulk of this.

Let me try reducing the RAM depth by using 8-entry arrays with explicit decode. The original transpose has 4 banks × 32 entries. The read address is 5 bits = {block, addr[3:0]}. If I use 4 banks × 2 halves × 8 entries, the read mux would be: 8:1 (3-bit addr) × 2:1 (half select) × 4 (bank select but all 4 read simultaneously). 

But actually the 4 banks are read simultaneously, so the mux is just 32:1 per bank. If I split into 4 sub-banks of 8 within each bank, I get 4:1 half select + 8:1 within = same 32:1 total but with 2 levels of logic instead of flat.

Let me try using an explicit mux structure with the 4-bit address split into two 2-bit halves:

Actually, let me try something completely different. The rd_addr_q uses QN (inverted) because the synthesis mapped it to an inverting DFF. What if I store the complement? Or what if I use a positive-logic DFF by changing the reset behavior?

Looking at the sequence, the address depends on rd_idx_q[2:0] and the current value. The case is:
- 3'd0: addr = addr - 8
- 3'd1: addr = addr + 8

- 3'd3: addr = addr - 8

- ...

idx 0: B, idx 1: B-8, idx 2: B, idx 3: B, idx 4: B-8, idx 5: B, idx 6: B-8, idx 7: B-7

Where B starts at 8 and increments by 1 each group. So B = 8 + (rd_idx >> 3).

addr = B + offset[rd_idx[2:0]] where:
- 0: 0, 1: -8, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: -8, 5: 0, 6: -8, 7: -7

- 0,2,3,5: addr = B
- 1,4,6: addr = B - 8
- 7: addr = B - 7

My direct computation is wrong. The issue is that the read uses the CURRENT rd_addr_q, not the NEXT one. So when I compute the next address from rd_idx_q, the current read still uses the old address. The issue: in the original, rd_addr_q is set in SETUP to 8, then in ACTIVE the read uses rd_addr_q (which is 8 for the first read) and computes the next value. In my version, I'm computing the next rd_addr_q from rd_idx_q, but the read still uses the CURRENT rd_addr_q. So:
- SETUP: rd_addr=8 (correct, first read uses 8)
- ACTIVE rd_idx=0: read uses rd_addr=8, next computed as 8+0=8 (WRONG, should be 0)

## Summary

### Best Result
`design_v3.v` with `target_delay=3000`: **8,950,623** area_delay_product (down from starting point's 9,496,575). The last edit to `design_v3.v` broke correctness (attempted direct address computation), so the actual best passing version is `design_v3.v` before that edit (the version evaluated at step 44, reverted at step 55).

### What Worked
1. **`ptr_conv` → bit-swap**: The `jpeg_idct_y` module had a 64-entry case-statement function that was just `{ptr_q[2:0], ptr_q[5:3]}`. Replacing with the direct expression saved area and delay.
2. **Inlining transpose RAM**: Removing the `jpeg_idct_transpose_ram` submodule wrapper and coding the 4 RAM arrays directly simplified the hierarchy.
3. **`target_delay=3000`**: Marginal area reduction (4270→4261) with no delay change.

### What Didn't Work (Regressions)
1. **Removing `mul_q→mul` pipeline stage** (v2, v5): Broke timing alignment — the valid_q depth and transpose/y-pass pipeline stages are tightly coupled. Outputs shifted by 1 cycle, causing mismatches.
2. **Splitting 32-entry RAMs into 16-entry halves** (v2): The 16:1 mux + 2:1 select was *worse* than a flat 32:1 mux (delay increased from 2100→2426ps).
3. **Pre-registering read address** (v7): Added a pipeline register that drove all 4 RAMs, increasing fan-out and DFF delay to 2664ps (total delay 3680ps). Much worse.
4. **Separate per-RAM address registers** (step 50): Same fan-out problem; the duplicated register had even higher load.
5. **Direct address computation from `rd_idx_q`** (steps 57-59): Formula was incorrect and ran out of steps to debug.

### Key Insight
The critical path was always `rd_addr_q (DFF QN, ~1400ps) → 32:1 RAM mux → ram_read_q (DFF)`. The DFF QN delay was high due to driving 128 mux inputs across 4 RAMs. Every attempt to break this path (pipeline registers, fan-out reduction) made things worse because the new register had equal or higher fan-out. The synthesis tool produced an essentially fixed netlist regardless of `target_delay`.

### What I'd Do Differently
1. **Restructure the transpose fundamentally**: Use 8 banks of 8 entries (8:1 mux) instead of 4 banks of 32 (32:1 mux), halving mux depth. This requires understanding the exact write/read address mapping.
2. **Merge transpose into y-pass**: Have the y-pass read the x-pass output buffer directly in transposed order, eliminating the separate transpose stage entirely.
3. **Fix the direct address computation**: The approach of computing `rd_addr_q` from `rd_idx_q` directly (eliminating sequential dependency) is promising but needs careful tracing of the read/write address mapping.

================================================================================
